I haven't really noticed myself doing that, I don't think. \n\nThough, talking about doing things out of nowhere, my dog Buster does that all the time. He's a hound mix I found wandering down by the old quarry. He'll just take off running after nothing at all. The other day he bolted right through Mrs. Gable's tomato garden. She was so mad. She grows those big beefsteak tomatoes, you know? The ones that are just perfect for a BLT sandwich. Man, I love a good BLT. You gotta toast the bread just right, though, or it gets all soggy from the mayo. I usually use that thick-cut bacon from the butcher in town. The guy who runs it always gives me a discount 'cause I helped him clear out some brush behind his property last summer. It was so hot that day, I thought I was gonna pass out...\n\nBut, uh... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, thinking things through. Yeah, I don't really know. I just kinda do what I do, I reckon.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting or standing still?\n\nPatient: Oh, yeah, definitely. Even when I'm really trying to be respectful, you know? Like if I'm at a funeral or something important. My leg is just bouncing a mile a minute, or my fingers are tapping on the chair. \n\nTapping... man, I used to tap on this old snare drum my buddy gave me. He found it at a yard sale over in Shelbyville. Shelbyville's got the best yard sales, I swear. People just throw away perfectly good stuff out there. I found a whole set of socket wrenches at one once for like two bucks. The 10-millimeter was missing, of course. It's always the 10-millimeter, ain't it? I swear there's a black hole somewhere just full of 10-millimeter sockets and left socks. I had this one pair of thick wool socks for the winter, kept my feet so warm when I was out shoveling snow, but I lost the left one in the wash. My washing machine makes this awful clanking noise anyway, probably ate the thing. I tried to take the back panel off to look last week, but the screws were all stripped...\n\nWait, what were you asking me? Oh, right, sitting still. Yeah, so if I ain't bouncing my leg or tapping the chair, I'm twisting something in my hands the whole time. A napkin, a paperclip, whatever I can grab. I just can't keep my hands and legs quiet.\n\nDoctor: Were you also really restless and had trouble sitting still when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I sure was. Back when I was a boy in school, it was the same thing. The teachers were always moving my seat around 'cause I just could not stay put. I was always talking out of turn, getting in trouble for jumping up before class was even done. \n\nJumping up... man, that reminds me of this trampoline my neighbor had when we were kids. Mr. Henderson, that was his name. He had this big old trampoline in his backyard, no safety net or nothing. We used to try to do flips on it, and one time my brother bounced right off and landed in Mrs. Henderson's prize rose bushes. Oh, she was furious. She had these beautiful yellow roses, won some ribbon at the county fair for 'em. The county fair is always a good time, ain't it? They have this one stand that sells deep-fried Oreos. Have you ever had a deep-fried Oreo? It sounds crazy, but it's like a warm, gooey chocolate cloud. I tried to make 'em at home once, but the batter got everywhere and the oil popped and burned my arm. I still got a little scar right here, see? It looks kind of like a crescent moon if you squint at it. \n\nBut, uh... where was I going with that? Oh, right, being a kid. Yeah, I was always getting in trouble for not sitting still and jumping up when I wasn't supposed to. Just couldn't help it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often get into fights or arguments with others?\n\nPatient: Fights? Arguments? No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I ain't an angry fella or nothing. I try to get along with folks best I can. \n\nGetting along... you know, that makes me think of my cousin Jimmy. He's the one who lives out by the lake. We used to go fishing out there all the time when we were kids. He had this little aluminum johnboat, and we'd take it out right around sunrise when the water was real still, like glass. Caught a bass out there once that must have been, I don't know, five pounds at least. Jimmy tried to tell everyone he caught it, but I know it was on my line. He's always doing that, taking credit for stuff. Like the time we built that treehouse in his backyard. I was the one who hauled all the lumber up there, but he told his dad he did all the heavy lifting. His dad is a carpenter, actually. Makes these real nice wooden rocking chairs. My mom bought one from him a few years back, keeps it on her front porch. It squeaks a little bit when you rock in it, though. I told her I'd come over and oil the joints for her, but I keep forgetting to pick up some WD-40 when I'm at the hardware store. The hardware store in town closed down, so now I gotta drive all the way out to the highway to get anything...\n\nWhat was the question again? Oh, fights. No, I don't really get into fights with people. I just mind my own business, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often do things without thinking about how they might affect you or others?\n\nPatient: Doing things without thinking? Nah, I don't think so. I mean, I usually try to plan things out, you know? Like, I'm not just jumping into stuff blind. \n\nJumping into stuff... that reminds me of this time me and my buddy Mike decided to fix up his old Ford Ranger. We actually sat down and made a whole list of parts we needed before we did anything. Went down to the auto parts store on 4th street. The guy working there, I think his name was Earl, he always wore these bright red suspenders. Looked just like a fireman. My uncle was a volunteer fireman for a while, actually. He drove the big ladder truck. He let me sit in it once when I was a kid, hit the siren and everything. Man, that thing was loud. Louder than my neighbor's dog, that's for sure. They got this little beagle that just howls at all hours of the night. I tried giving it a hotdog over the fence to keep it quiet last week, but it just ate the hotdog and barked louder. Hotdogs are getting so expensive though, ain't they? I went to the grocery store yesterday and a pack of the good beef ones was like six bucks. Six bucks! I ended up just buying bologna instead. You can fry bologna up in a pan with a little butter, put it on white bread with some mustard... best sandwich in the world. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Something about doing things? Oh, right, without thinking. Yeah, no, I don't really do that. I usually know what I'm doing and think about it first.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind wandering and trouble following instructions is more of a problem for you than the feeling of restlessness and need to move?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if one is worse than the other, really. They both just kind of mess everything up. Like, I mean well, I really do, but people at church, the food pantry where I volunteer, even my own family... they've just stopped trusting me with things. They won't ask me for rides anymore, or to help with appointments, or even to pick up supplies. I feel like I just keep letting everybody down, even when I'm really trying to help.\n\nTrying to help... man, that reminds me of this time I tried to help my buddy fix his roof. He had this little leak right above his kitchen sink. We got up there with a bucket of tar, and I accidentally knocked the whole thing over. Tar went everywhere, all down the side of the house, ruined his wife's petunias. She loves those petunias, plants 'em every spring. Spring is my favorite time of year, honestly. The weather finally warms up, and the baseball season starts. I used to play baseball when I was younger, played right field. I had this glove, this old leather Rawlings glove that my grandpa gave me. He bought it at a garage sale for like two dollars. Garage sales are great, you can find all sorts of stuff. Last week I found this old toaster oven, one of those retro ones from the 70s, bright orange. I brought it home to make toast, but the heating element was busted. So now it just sits on my counter looking pretty. \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, my mind wandering and the restlessness. Yeah, it's just... it's hard, you know? Feeling like you can't be counted on.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I do. I just jump in with an answer before people are even done talking. Like the pastor, or my cousin, or the pantry lady... I just blurt it out, and then I realize I answered the wrong thing entirely or just made 'em mad. \n\nMade 'em mad... that makes me think of Mrs. Higgins at the pantry. She gets so mad if the soup cans ain't lined up right on the shelf. She wants every single label facing the exact same way. Reminds me of this guy I used to work for at a grocery store back when I was a teenager. He wore these thick glasses that made his eyes look huge, like a bug. We used to call him the Praying Mantis. Praying mantises are crazy bugs, though. I saw one on my porch last summer grab a moth right out of the air. Just snatched it. My porch light attracts all kinds of bugs in the summer. I tried putting up one of those blue bug zappers, but the noise it makes is just awful. Zzzap, zzzap. Sounds like a bad wire. I had a bad wire in my truck's radio once, meant I could only pick up AM stations. I ended up listening to a lot of talk radio back then... mostly guys calling in to complain about the weather. The weather's been so weird lately, ain't it? Supposed to rain all next week, I heard. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Something about talking? Oh, interrupting. Yeah, I just jump in before they're done. It just slips out before I can stop it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you have more trouble with paying attention or with sitting still and being quiet?\n\nPatient: Sitting still or paying attention? Man, I don't know, they're both pretty bad. Maybe the sitting still part? I just feel like I got this motor in me that won't shut off, you know? Like I always gotta be doing something or moving around. \n\nMoving around... that reminds me of this old rocking chair my grandma had. She'd sit on the porch and just rock back and forth all afternoon. It had this squeak to it, real high-pitched. Squeak, squeak. Drove my grandpa crazy. He tried oiling it, taking it apart, everything. Nothing worked. He was pretty handy, though. Built a whole shed in the backyard out of scrap wood he found at the dump. The dump is a wild place if you really look around. People throw away perfectly good stuff! I found a whole set of golf clubs there once. Never played golf in my life, but I figured I might try it. Took 'em to the park and ended up hitting a ball right through the window of this guy's minivan. He was not happy. Had to pay him like two hundred bucks to fix it. Two hundred bucks is a lot of money when you don't got a job, let me tell you. I try to save where I can, clip coupons and stuff. The Sunday paper always has the best coupons. I like reading the comics, too. Garfield is my favorite. He just hates Mondays and loves lasagna. \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, sitting still. Yeah, I don't know, I just can't seem to stay put for very long. It's like I'm always itching to get up and go somewhere else.\n\nDoctor: Do you often do things on impulse without considering the consequences?\n\nPatient: Impulse? Consequences? I ain't really sure what you mean by that, honestly. Like, I don't know if I do things without thinking. Maybe? I guess I just haven't really noticed if I do. Like buying stuff on impulse? Sometimes I grab a candy bar at the checkout line, if that counts. Usually a Snickers. I love the peanuts in 'em. Peanuts always make me think of going to baseball games with my uncle... well, minor league games, down at the old stadium before they tore it down. They had this mascot, a giant chicken or something. The guy inside that suit must've been sweating bullets, 'cause it gets so hot here in July. July is when my birthday is, actually. I didn't do much this year, just had a hot dog and watched TV. TV ain't what it used to be, though. It's all these reality shows now. I saw one about guys digging for gold up in Alaska. Alaska looks freezing, man. I don't think I could handle the cold up there, my knees start aching the second it drops below fifty degrees here. The free clinic doc told me it's just from getting older, but I ain't even forty yet. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Something about doing things on impulse? Yeah, I don't really know. I haven't really noticed doing anything like that, to be honest.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself doing things without fully thinking them through?\n\nPatient: Thinking them through? I don't know, maybe. I don't really think about how I think, if that makes sense. It's just... I do what I do. Like yesterday, I just started walking. Ended up down by the river. \n\nThe river's been real high lately, muddy too. Saw this guy fishing down there, had a whole setup with three poles and a little cooler. I asked him what he was catching, and he said catfish, mostly. Catfish are weird-looking, ain't they? With those whiskers. My buddy used to have a pet catfish in a big tank in his living room. It ate everything. He'd drop hot dogs in there, pieces of cheese, whatever. He had this other tank with a turtle, a red-eared slider. That turtle was mean, tried to bite my finger once. Fingers bleed a lot when you cut 'em, you ever notice that? I sliced mine open trying to open a can of baked beans last week. The pull-tab broke off, so I was using a screwdriver to pry it open. Stupid, I know, but I was hungry. Baked beans are best if you put a little mustard in 'em. My mom taught me that. She used to make this huge pot of beans for the church picnics every summer. Church picnics always had three-legged races. I was never any good at those, my legs are too long or something. I'm pretty tall, almost six-two. \n\nHold on, what were we talking about? Doing things without thinking? Yeah, I don't know. I guess I just do things when they come to me. I don't really sit around planning stuff out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you have more trouble with staying focused on tasks or with controlling your physical activity and restlessness?\n\nPatient: Staying focused or the restlessness? Man, that's tough. I mean, the moving around is bad, but the focus thing... yeah, that's a real problem too. Like, when folks are talking to me about stuff, I try to listen, I really do. Like if someone's running through the ride times for the bus, or reading off a pantry list, or even going over the plans for Sunday at church. I start out listening good, but then my mind just sort of... slips off. \n\nSlips off... like this time I was trying to fix the chain on my buddy's bike. It kept slipping off the gears. He had this old ten-speed, Schwinn I think it was. Had those curved handlebars that make you lean way over. I never liked those, hurts my back. My lower back has been killing me lately. I think it's from sleeping on this old mattress I got from my neighbor. He was moving to Florida, going to live with his daughter. Florida's got too many bugs for me. Palmetto bugs, they call 'em, but they're just giant cockroaches if you ask me. I saw one on TV once that was the size of a mouse. Mice are smart, though. You ever try to catch one? They figure out the traps real quick. You gotta use peanut butter, not cheese. Cheese dries out too fast. I like cheddar cheese on my eggs in the morning. Scrambled, with a little hot sauce. Hot sauce makes everything better. I got this bottle of habanero sauce that'll burn your tongue right off. \n\nAnyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, the focus thing. Yeah, my mind just wanders off, and then suddenly I realize I missed the pieces I really needed to hear. Like, I won't know what time the bus is coming or what I was supposed to bring to the church thing. It's frustrating, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your trouble with following instructions and paying attention is more of a problem for you than your restlessness and need to move?\n\nPatient: Following instructions? Man, I don't know if it's worse than the moving around, but it definitely gets me in trouble. Like, I’ll start an errand for my aunt or a job down at the food pantry, right? And I start out strong, but I end up doing just the first part and then... I just forget the rest. So people find out later that I never finished it. \n\nLike last week, my aunt asked me to go to the hardware store for some lightbulbs and a new air filter. Hardware stores are great, all those tools and stuff. They had this whole display of power washers right up front. I always wanted a power washer. You ever watch those videos of people power washing dirty driveways? It's real satisfying, just blasting all the grime away. Grime gets everywhere around here, especially with all the cars going by. The street sweepers come through on Tuesdays, but they don't do much. They just push the dirt around. I used to sweep the sidewalk in front of my old apartment building, just to keep it looking nice. The landlord there was a real piece of work, though. Never fixed anything. The radiator in my bedroom hissed all winter. Sounded like a snake. Snakes give me the creeps. Saw a garter snake in the park once, near the bushes. It was just a little guy, but I still jumped back. \n\nWait, what was the question? Instructions? Yeah, so I got the lightbulbs, but I completely forgot the air filter. Didn't even realize it until I got back and my aunt asked where it was. It just completely slipped my mind after I saw the power washers.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your trouble with paying attention is worse than your trouble with sitting still and being quiet?\n\nPatient: Worse than sitting still? I don't know if it's worse, man. The sitting still part is... it's like a physical thing, you know? Like, I just can't do it. If I'm in a meeting down at the community center, or eating dinner with my aunt, or even just sitting in a waiting room like out there... I just keep standing up. I gotta go get water, or I go look out the window to see what's happening outside, or I just pace around a little. Even when everyone else is just sitting there, quiet, staring at me like I'm crazy. \n\nStaring at me... people stare a lot. Like this guy at the bus stop the other day, just staring at my shoes. I got these new sneakers, bright orange. Orange is a good color, easy to see in the dark. You gotta be careful walking around at night, people drive like maniacs. My cousin got hit by a car once, broke his leg in three places. He had this massive cast on for months. Everyone signed it. I drew a little picture of a dog on it. I always wanted a dog, like a big one, a German Shepherd maybe. They use those for police dogs, right? Smart dogs. I saw a police dog sniff out a whole bag of stuff hidden in a car bumper on one of those cop shows. Those shows are intense. All the sirens and running. \n\nBut yeah, what were you asking? The sitting still thing? Yeah, it's bad. I just can't stay in my chair. I'm always up and down, up and down, getting water or just moving. It's like my legs have a mind of their own.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

So now it just sits on my counter looking pretty. \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, my mind wandering and the restlessness. Yeah, it's just... it's hard, you know? Feeling like you can't be counted on.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I do. I just jump in with an answer before people are even done talking. Like the pastor, or my cousin, or the pantry lady... I just blurt it out, and then I realize I answered the wrong thing entirely or just made 'em mad. \n\nMade 'em mad... that makes me think of Mrs. Higgins at the pantry. She gets so mad if the soup cans ain't lined up right on the shelf. She wants every single label facing the exact same way. Reminds me of this guy I used to work for at a grocery store back when I was a teenager. He wore these thick glasses that made his eyes look huge, like a bug. We used to call him the Praying Mantis. Praying mantises are crazy bugs, though. I saw one on my porch last summer grab a moth right out of the air. Just snatched it. My porch light attracts all kinds of bugs in the summer. I tried putting up one of those blue bug zappers, but the noise it makes is just awful. Zzzap, zzzap. Sounds like a bad wire. I had a bad wire in my truck's radio once, meant I could only pick up AM stations. I ended up listening to a lot of talk radio back then... mostly guys calling in to complain about the weather. The weather's been so weird lately, ain't it? Supposed to rain all next week, I heard. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Something about talking? Oh, interrupting. Yeah, I just jump in before they're done. It just slips out before I can stop it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you have more trouble with paying attention or with sitting still and being quiet?\n\nPatient: Sitting still or paying attention? Man, I don't know, they're both pretty bad. Maybe the sitting still part? I just feel like I got this motor in me that won't shut off, you know? Like I always gotta be doing something or moving around. \n\nMoving around... that reminds me of this old rocking chair my grandma had. She'd sit on the porch and just rock back and forth all afternoon. It had this squeak to it, real high-pitched. Squeak, squeak. Drove my grandpa crazy. He tried oiling it, taking it apart, everything. Nothing worked. He was pretty handy, though. Built a whole shed in the backyard out of scrap wood he found at the dump. The dump is a wild place if you really look around. People throw away perfectly good stuff! I found a whole set of golf clubs there once. Never played golf in my life, but I figured I might try it. Took 'em to the park and ended up hitting a ball right through the window of this guy's minivan. He was not happy. Had to pay him like two hundred bucks to fix it. Two hundred bucks is a lot of money when you don't got a job, let me tell you. I try to save where I can, clip coupons and stuff. The Sunday paper always has the best coupons. I like reading the comics, too. Garfield is my favorite. He just hates Mondays and loves lasagna. \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, sitting still. Yeah, I don't know, I just can't seem to stay put for very long. It's like I'm always itching to get up and go somewhere else.\n\nDoctor: Do you often do things on impulse without considering the consequences?\n\nPatient: Impulse? Consequences? I ain't really sure what you mean by that, honestly. Like, I don't know if I do things without thinking. Maybe? I guess I just haven't really noticed if I do. Like buying stuff on impulse? Sometimes I grab a candy bar at the checkout line, if that counts. Usually a Snickers. I love the peanuts in 'em. Peanuts always make me think of going to baseball games with my uncle... well, minor league games, down at the old stadium before they tore it down. They had this mascot, a giant chicken or something. The guy inside that suit must've been sweating bullets, 'cause it gets so hot here in July. July is when my birthday is, actually. I didn't do much this year, just had a hot dog and watched TV. TV ain't what it used to be, though. It's all these reality shows now. I saw one about guys digging for gold up in Alaska. Alaska looks freezing, man. I don't think I could handle the cold up there, my knees start aching the second it drops below fifty degrees here. The free clinic doc told me it's just from getting older, but I ain't even forty yet. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Something about doing things on impulse? Yeah, I don't really know. I haven't really noticed doing anything like that, to be honest.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself doing things without fully thinking them through?\n\nPatient: Thinking them through? I don't know, maybe. I don't really think about how I think, if that makes sense. It's just... I do what I do. Like yesterday, I just started walking. Ended up down by the river. \n\nThe river's been real high lately, muddy too. Saw this guy fishing down there, had a whole setup with three poles and a little cooler. I asked him what he was catching, and he said catfish, mostly. Catfish are weird-looking, ain't they? With those whiskers. My buddy used to have a pet catfish in a big tank in his living room. It ate everything. He'd drop hot dogs in there, pieces of cheese, whatever. He had this other tank with a turtle, a red-eared slider. That turtle was mean, tried to bite my finger once. Fingers bleed a lot when you cut 'em, you ever notice that? I sliced mine open trying to open a can of baked beans last week. The pull-tab broke off, so I was using a screwdriver to pry it open. Stupid, I know, but I was hungry. Baked beans are best if you put a little mustard in 'em. My mom taught me that. She used to make this huge pot of beans for the church picnics every summer. Church picnics always had three-legged races. I was never any good at those, my legs are too long or something. I'm pretty tall, almost six-two. \n\nHold on, what were we talking about? Doing things without thinking? Yeah, I don't know. I guess I just do things when they come to me. I don't really sit around planning stuff out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you have more trouble with staying focused on tasks or with controlling your physical activity and restlessness?\n\nPatient: Staying focused or the restlessness? Man, that's tough. I mean, the moving around is bad, but the focus thing... yeah, that's a real problem too. Like, when folks are talking to me about stuff, I try to listen, I really do. Like if someone's running through the ride times for the bus, or reading off a pantry list, or even going over the plans for Sunday at church. I start out listening good, but then my mind just sort of... slips off. \n\nSlips off... like this time I was trying to fix the chain on my buddy's bike. It kept slipping off the gears. He had this old ten-speed, Schwinn I think it was. Had those curved handlebars that make you lean way over. I never liked those, hurts my back. My lower back has been killing me lately. I think it's from sleeping on this old mattress I got from my neighbor. He was moving to Florida, going to live with his daughter. Florida's got too many bugs for me. Palmetto bugs, they call 'em, but they're just giant cockroaches if you ask me. I saw one on TV once that was the size of a mouse. Mice are smart, though. You ever try to catch one? They figure out the traps real quick. You gotta use peanut butter, not cheese. Cheese dries out too fast. I like cheddar cheese on my eggs in the morning. Scrambled, with a little hot sauce. Hot sauce makes everything better. I got this bottle of habanero sauce that'll burn your tongue right off. \n\nAnyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, the focus thing. Yeah, my mind just wanders off, and then suddenly I realize I missed the pieces I really needed to hear. Like, I won't know what time the bus is coming or what I was supposed to bring to the church thing. It's frustrating, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your trouble with following instructions and paying attention is more of a problem for you than your restlessness and need to move?\n\nPatient: Following instructions? Man, I don't know if it's worse than the moving around, but it definitely gets me in trouble. Like, I’ll start an errand for my aunt or a job down at the food pantry, right? And I start out strong, but I end up doing just the first part and then... I just forget the rest. So people find out later that I never finished it. \n\nLike last week, my aunt asked me to go to the hardware store for some lightbulbs and a new air filter. Hardware stores are great, all those tools and stuff. They had this whole display of power washers right up front. I always wanted a power washer. You ever watch those videos of people power washing dirty driveways? It's real satisfying, just blasting all the grime away. Grime gets everywhere around here, especially with all the cars going by. The street sweepers come through on Tuesdays, but they don't do much. They just push the dirt around. I used to sweep the sidewalk in front of my old apartment building, just to keep it looking nice. The landlord there was a real piece of work, though. Never fixed anything. The radiator in my bedroom hissed all winter. Sounded like a snake. Snakes give me the creeps. Saw a garter snake in the park once, near the bushes. It was just a little guy, but I still jumped back. \n\nWait, what was the question? Instructions? Yeah, so I got the lightbulbs, but I completely forgot the air filter. Didn't even realize it until I got back and my aunt asked where it was. It just completely slipped my mind after I saw the power washers.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your trouble with paying attention is worse than your trouble with sitting still and being quiet?\n\nPatient: Worse than sitting still? I don't know if it's worse, man. The sitting still part is... it's like a physical thing, you know? Like, I just can't do it. If I'm in a meeting down at the community center, or eating dinner with my aunt, or even just sitting in a waiting room like out there... I just keep standing up. I gotta go get water, or I go look out the window to see what's happening outside, or I just pace around a little. Even when everyone else is just sitting there, quiet, staring at me like I'm crazy. \n\nStaring at me... people stare a lot. Like this guy at the bus stop the other day, just staring at my shoes. I got these new sneakers, bright orange. Orange is a good color, easy to see in the dark. You gotta be careful walking around at night, people drive like maniacs. My cousin got hit by a car once, broke his leg in three places. He had this massive cast on for months. Everyone signed it. I drew a little picture of a dog on it. I always wanted a dog, like a big one, a German Shepherd maybe. They use those for police dogs, right? Smart dogs. I saw a police dog sniff out a whole bag of stuff hidden in a car bumper on one of those cop shows. Those shows are intense. All the sirens and running. \n\nBut yeah, what were you asking? The sitting still thing? Yeah, it's bad. I just can't stay in my chair. I'm always up and down, up and down, getting water or just moving. It's like my legs have a mind of their own.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Staying focused or fidgeting? Man, they're both just... they're always there. But the fidgeting, it's like I can't even stop it if I try. Even when I'm really trying to be respectful, like if I'm talking to my aunt's pastor or something, my leg is just bouncing up and down, up and down. Or my fingers are tapping on the chair, or I'm twisting whatever I can get my hands on, like a pen or a piece of paper. 

Paper... my aunt has all these old newspapers stacked up in the corner of the living room. She says she's gonna read 'em, but she never does. They just sit there collecting dust. Dust is bad for your lungs, you know? I saw this documentary about coal miners and how they get that black lung disease. Sounds awful. Being down in a mine all day, in the dark, with all that dust. I don't like tight spaces anyway. I got stuck in an elevator once for like, two hours. Just me and this lady who kept talking about her cats. She had six cats. Six! That's too many cats. The smell alone... I can't stand the smell of litter boxes. 

But yeah, the fidgeting. It's like my body just has to keep moving, even when I'm trying my hardest to just sit there and be still. My hands or my legs are always going.

Man, I don't know if it's worse than the moving around, but it definitely gets me in trouble. Like, I’ll start an errand for my aunt or a job down at the food pantry, right? The street sweepers come through on Tuesdays, but they don't do much. They just push the dirt around. I used to sweep the sidewalk in front of my old apartment building, just to keep it looking nice. The landlord there was a real piece of work, though. Never fixed anything. The radiator in my bedroom hissed all winter. Sounded like a snake. Snakes give me the creeps. Saw a garter snake in the park once, near the bushes. It was just a little guy, but I still jumped back. \n\nWait, what was the question? Instructions? Yeah, so I got the lightbulbs, but I completely forgot the air filter. Didn't even realize it until I got back and my aunt asked where it was. It just completely slipped my mind after I saw the power washers.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your trouble with paying attention is worse than your trouble with sitting still and being quiet?\n\nPatient: Worse than sitting still? I don't know if it's worse, man. The sitting still part is... it's like a physical thing, you know? Like, I just can't do it. If I'm in a meeting down at the community center, or eating dinner with my aunt, or even just sitting in a waiting room like out there... I just keep standing up. I gotta go get water, or I go look out the window to see what's happening outside, or I just pace around a little. Even when everyone else is just sitting there, quiet, staring at me like I'm crazy. \n\nStaring at me... people stare a lot. Like this guy at the bus stop the other day, just staring at my shoes. I got these new sneakers, bright orange. Orange is a good color, easy to see in the dark. You gotta be careful walking around at night, people drive like maniacs. My cousin got hit by a car once, broke his leg in three places. He had this massive cast on for months. Everyone signed it. I drew a little picture of a dog on it. I always wanted a dog, like a big one, a German Shepherd maybe. They use those for police dogs, right? Smart dogs. I saw a police dog sniff out a whole bag of stuff hidden in a car bumper on one of those cop shows. Those shows are intense. All the sirens and running. \n\nBut yeah, what were you asking? The sitting still thing? Yeah, it's bad. I just can't stay in my chair. I'm always up and down, up and down, getting water or just moving. It's like my legs have a mind of their own.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you have more trouble with staying focused on what people are saying or with staying still and not fidgeting?\n\nPatient: Staying focused or fidgeting? I tried to take the back panel off to look last week, but the screws were all stripped...\n\nWait, what were you asking me? Oh, right, sitting still. Yeah, so if I ain't bouncing my leg or tapping the chair, I'm twisting something in my hands the whole time. A napkin, a paperclip, whatever I can grab. I just can't keep my hands and legs quiet.\n\nDoctor: Were you also really restless and had trouble sitting still when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I sure was. Back when I was a boy in school, it was the same thing. The teachers were always moving my seat around 'cause I just could not stay put. I was always talking out of turn, getting in trouble for jumping up before class was even done. \n\nJumping up... man, that reminds me of this trampoline my neighbor had when we were kids. Mr. Henderson, that was his name. He had this big old trampoline in his backyard, no safety net or nothing. We used to try to do flips on it, and one time my brother bounced right off and landed in Mrs. Henderson's prize rose bushes. Oh, she was furious. She had these beautiful yellow roses, won some ribbon at the county fair for 'em. The county fair is always a good time, ain't it? They have this one stand that sells deep-fried Oreos. Have you ever had a deep-fried Oreo? It sounds crazy, but it's like a warm, gooey chocolate cloud. I tried to make 'em at home once, but the batter got everywhere and the oil popped and burned my arm. I still got a little scar right here, see? It looks kind of like a crescent moon if you squint at it. \n\nBut, uh... where was I going with that? Oh, right, being a kid. Yeah, I was always getting in trouble for not sitting still and jumping up when I wasn't supposed to. Just couldn't help it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often get into fights or arguments with others?\n\nPatient: Fights? Arguments? No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I ain't an angry fella or nothing. I try to get along with folks best I can. \n\nGetting along... you know, that makes me think of my cousin Jimmy. He's the one who lives out by the lake. We used to go fishing out there all the time when we were kids. He had this little aluminum johnboat, and we'd take it out right around sunrise when the water was real still, like glass. Caught a bass out there once that must have been, I don't know, five pounds at least. Jimmy tried to tell everyone he caught it, but I know it was on my line. He's always doing that, taking credit for stuff. Like the time we built that treehouse in his backyard. I was the one who hauled all the lumber up there, but he told his dad he did all the heavy lifting. His dad is a carpenter, actually. Makes these real nice wooden rocking chairs. My mom bought one from him a few years back, keeps it on her front porch. It squeaks a little bit when you rock in it, though. I told her I'd come over and oil the joints for her, but I keep forgetting to pick up some WD-40 when I'm at the hardware store. The hardware store in town closed down, so now I gotta drive all the way out to the highway to get anything...\n\nWhat was the question again? Oh, fights. No, I don't really get into fights with people. I just mind my own business, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often do things without thinking about how they might affect you or others?\n\nPatient: Doing things without thinking? Nah, I don't think so. I mean, I usually try to plan things out, you know? Like, I'm not just jumping into stuff blind. \n\nJumping into stuff... that reminds me of this time me and my buddy Mike decided to fix up his old Ford Ranger. We actually sat down and made a whole list of parts we needed before we did anything. Went down to the auto parts store on 4th street. The guy working there, I think his name was Earl, he always wore these bright red suspenders. Looked just like a fireman. My uncle was a volunteer fireman for a while, actually. He drove the big ladder truck. He let me sit in it once when I was a kid, hit the siren and everything. Man, that thing was loud. Louder than my neighbor's dog, that's for sure. They got this little beagle that just howls at all hours of the night. I tried giving it a hotdog over the fence to keep it quiet last week, but it just ate the hotdog and barked louder. Hotdogs are getting so expensive though, ain't they? I went to the grocery store yesterday and a pack of the good beef ones was like six bucks. Six bucks! I ended up just buying bologna instead. You can fry bologna up in a pan with a little butter, put it on white bread with some mustard... best sandwich in the world. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Something about doing things? Oh, right, without thinking. Yeah, no, I don't really do that. I usually know what I'm doing and think about it first.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind wandering and trouble following instructions is more of a problem for you than the feeling of restlessness and need to move?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if one is worse than the other, really. They both just kind of mess everything up. Like, I mean well, I really do, but people at church, the food pantry where I volunteer, even my own family... they've just stopped trusting me with things. They won't ask me for rides anymore, or to help with appointments, or even to pick up supplies. I feel like I just keep letting everybody down, even when I'm really trying to help.\n\nTrying to help... man, that reminds me of this time I tried to help my buddy fix his roof. He had this little leak right above his kitchen sink. We got up there with a bucket of tar, and I accidentally knocked the whole thing over. Tar went everywhere, all down the side of the house, ruined his wife's petunias. She loves those petunias, plants 'em every spring. Spring is my favorite time of year, honestly. The weather finally warms up, and the baseball season starts. I used to play baseball when I was younger, played right field. I had this glove, this old leather Rawlings glove that my grandpa gave me. He bought it at a garage sale for like two dollars. Garage sales are great, you can find all sorts of stuff. Last week I found this old toaster oven, one of those retro ones from the 70s, bright orange. I brought it home to make toast, but the heating element was busted. So now it just sits on my counter looking pretty. \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, my mind wandering and the restlessness. Yeah, it's just... it's hard, you know? Feeling like you can't be counted on.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I do. I just jump in with an answer before people are even done talking. Like the pastor, or my cousin, or the pantry lady... I just blurt it out, and then I realize I answered the wrong thing entirely or just made 'em mad. \n\nMade 'em mad... that makes me think of Mrs. Higgins at the pantry. She gets so mad if the soup cans ain't lined up right on the shelf. She wants every single label facing the exact same way. Reminds me of this guy I used to work for at a grocery store back when I was a teenager. He wore these thick glasses that made his eyes look huge, like a bug. We used to call him the Praying Mantis. Praying mantises are crazy bugs, though. I saw one on my porch last summer grab a moth right out of the air. Just snatched it. My porch light attracts all kinds of bugs in the summer. I tried putting up one of those blue bug zappers, but the noise it makes is just awful. Zzzap, zzzap. Sounds like a bad wire. I had a bad wire in my truck's radio once, meant I could only pick up AM stations. I ended up listening to a lot of talk radio back then... mostly guys calling in to complain about the weather. The weather's been so weird lately, ain't it? Supposed to rain all next week, I heard. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Something about talking? Oh, interrupting. Yeah, I just jump in before they're done. It just slips out before I can stop it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you have more trouble with paying attention or with sitting still and being quiet?\n\nPatient: Sitting still or paying attention? Man, I don't know, they're both pretty bad. Maybe the sitting still part? I just feel like I got this motor in me that won't shut off, you know? Like I always gotta be doing something or moving around. \n\nMoving around... that reminds me of this old rocking chair my grandma had. She'd sit on the porch and just rock back and forth all afternoon. It had this squeak to it, real high-pitched. Squeak, squeak. Drove my grandpa crazy. He tried oiling it, taking it apart, everything. Nothing worked. He was pretty handy, though. Built a whole shed in the backyard out of scrap wood he found at the dump. The dump is a wild place if you really look around. People throw away perfectly good stuff! I found a whole set of golf clubs there once. Never played golf in my life, but I figured I might try it. Took 'em to the park and ended up hitting a ball right through the window of this guy's minivan. He was not happy. Had to pay him like two hundred bucks to fix it. Two hundred bucks is a lot of money when you don't got a job, let me tell you. I try to save where I can, clip coupons and stuff. The Sunday paper always has the best coupons. I like reading the comics, too. Garfield is my favorite. He just hates Mondays and loves lasagna. \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, sitting still. Yeah, I don't know, I just can't seem to stay put for very long. It's like I'm always itching to get up and go somewhere else.\n\nDoctor: Do you often do things on impulse without considering the consequences?\n\nPatient: Impulse? Consequences? I ain't really sure what you mean by that, honestly. Like, I don't know if I do things without thinking. Maybe? I guess I just haven't really noticed if I do. Like buying stuff on impulse? Sometimes I grab a candy bar at the checkout line, if that counts. Usually a Snickers. I love the peanuts in 'em. Peanuts always make me think of going to baseball games with my uncle... well, minor league games, down at the old stadium before they tore it down. They had this mascot, a giant chicken or something. The guy inside that suit must've been sweating bullets, 'cause it gets so hot here in July. July is when my birthday is, actually. I didn't do much this year, just had a hot dog and watched TV. TV ain't what it used to be, though. It's all these reality shows now. I saw one about guys digging for gold up in Alaska. Alaska looks freezing, man. I don't think I could handle the cold up there, my knees start aching the second it drops below fifty degrees here. The free clinic doc told me it's just from getting older, but I ain't even forty yet. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Something about doing things on impulse? Yeah, I don't really know. I haven't really noticed doing anything like that, to be honest.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself doing things without fully thinking them through?\n\nPatient: Thinking them through? I don't know, maybe. I don't really think about how I think, if that makes sense. It's just... I do what I do. Like yesterday, I just started walking. Ended up down by the river. \n\nThe river's been real high lately, muddy too. Saw this guy fishing down there, had a whole setup with three poles and a little cooler. I asked him what he was catching, and he said catfish, mostly. Catfish are weird-looking, ain't they? With those whiskers. My buddy used to have a pet catfish in a big tank in his living room. It ate everything. He'd drop hot dogs in there, pieces of cheese, whatever. He had this other tank with a turtle, a red-eared slider. That turtle was mean, tried to bite my finger once. Fingers bleed a lot when you cut 'em, you ever notice that? I sliced mine open trying to open a can of baked beans last week. The pull-tab broke off, so I was using a screwdriver to pry it open. Stupid, I know, but I was hungry. Baked beans are best if you put a little mustard in 'em. My mom taught me that. She used to make this huge pot of beans for the church picnics every summer. Church picnics always had three-legged races. I was never any good at those, my legs are too long or something. I'm pretty tall, almost six-two. \n\nHold on, what were we talking about? Doing things without thinking? Yeah, I don't know. I guess I just do things when they come to me. I don't really sit around planning stuff out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you have more trouble with staying focused on tasks or with controlling your physical activity and restlessness?\n\nPatient: Staying focused or the restlessness? Man, that's tough. I mean, the moving around is bad, but the focus thing... yeah, that's a real problem too. Like, when folks are talking to me about stuff, I try to listen, I really do. Like if someone's running through the ride times for the bus, or reading off a pantry list, or even going over the plans for Sunday at church. I start out listening good, but then my mind just sort of... slips off. \n\nSlips off... like this time I was trying to fix the chain on my buddy's bike. It kept slipping off the gears. He had this old ten-speed, Schwinn I think it was. Had those curved handlebars that make you lean way over. I never liked those, hurts my back. My lower back has been killing me lately. I think it's from sleeping on this old mattress I got from my neighbor. He was moving to Florida, going to live with his daughter. Florida's got too many bugs for me. Palmetto bugs, they call 'em, but they're just giant cockroaches if you ask me. I saw one on TV once that was the size of a mouse. Mice are smart, though. You ever try to catch one? They figure out the traps real quick. You gotta use peanut butter, not cheese. Cheese dries out too fast. I like cheddar cheese on my eggs in the morning. Scrambled, with a little hot sauce. Hot sauce makes everything better. I got this bottle of habanero sauce that'll burn your tongue right off. \n\nAnyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, the focus thing. Yeah, my mind just wanders off, and then suddenly I realize I missed the pieces I really needed to hear. Like, I won't know what time the bus is coming or what I was supposed to bring to the church thing. It's frustrating, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your trouble with following instructions and paying attention is more of a problem for you than your restlessness and need to move?\n\nPatient: Following instructions? Man, I don't know if it's worse than the moving around, but it definitely gets me in trouble. Like, I’ll start an errand for my aunt or a job down at the food pantry, right? And I start out strong, but I end up doing just the first part and then... I just forget the rest. So people find out later that I never finished it. \n\nLike last week, my aunt asked me to go to the hardware store for some lightbulbs and a new air filter. Hardware stores are great, all those tools and stuff. They had this whole display of power washers right up front. I always wanted a power washer. You ever watch those videos of people power washing dirty driveways? It's real satisfying, just blasting all the grime away. Grime gets everywhere around here, especially with all the cars going by. The street sweepers come through on Tuesdays, but they don't do much. They just push the dirt around. I used to sweep the sidewalk in front of my old apartment building, just to keep it looking nice. The landlord there was a real piece of work, though. Never fixed anything. The radiator in my bedroom hissed all winter. Sounded like a snake. Snakes give me the creeps. Saw a garter snake in the park once, near the bushes. It was just a little guy, but I still jumped back. \n\nWait, what was the question? Instructions? Yeah, so I got the lightbulbs, but I completely forgot the air filter. Didn't even realize it until I got back and my aunt asked where it was. It just completely slipped my mind after I saw the power washers.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your trouble with paying attention is worse than your trouble with sitting still and being quiet?\n\nPatient: Worse than sitting still? I don't know if it's worse, man. The sitting still part is... it's like a physical thing, you know? Like, I just can't do it. If I'm in a meeting down at the community center, or eating dinner with my aunt, or even just sitting in a waiting room like out there... I just keep standing up. I gotta go get water, or I go look out the window to see what's happening outside, or I just pace around a little. Even when everyone else is just sitting there, quiet, staring at me like I'm crazy. \n\nStaring at me... people stare a lot. Like this guy at the bus stop the other day, just staring at my shoes. I got these new sneakers, bright orange. Orange is a good color, easy to see in the dark. You gotta be careful walking around at night, people drive like maniacs. My cousin got hit by a car once, broke his leg in three places. He had this massive cast on for months. Everyone signed it. I drew a little picture of a dog on it. I always wanted a dog, like a big one, a German Shepherd maybe. They use those for police dogs, right? Smart dogs. I saw a police dog sniff out a whole bag of stuff hidden in a car bumper on one of those cop shows. Those shows are intense. All the sirens and running. \n\nBut yeah, what were you asking? The sitting still thing? Yeah, it's bad. I just can't stay in my chair. I'm always up and down, up and down, getting water or just moving. It's like my legs have a mind of their own.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you have more trouble with staying focused on what people are saying or with staying still and not fidgeting?\n\nPatient: Staying focused or fidgeting? Man, they're both just... they're always there. But the fidgeting, it's like I can't even stop it if I try. Even when I'm really trying to be respectful, like if I'm talking to my aunt's pastor or something, my leg is just bouncing up and down, up and down. Or my fingers are tapping on the chair, or I'm twisting whatever I can get my hands on, like a pen or a piece of paper. \n\nPaper... my aunt has all these old newspapers stacked up in the corner of the living room. She says she's gonna read 'em, but she never does. They just sit there collecting dust. Dust is bad for your lungs, you know? I saw this documentary about coal miners and how they get that black lung disease. Sounds awful. Being down in a mine all day, in the dark, with all that dust. I don't like tight spaces anyway. I got stuck in an elevator once for like, two hours. Just me and this lady who kept talking about her cats. She had six cats. Six! That's too many cats. The smell alone... I can't stand the smell of litter boxes. \n\nBut yeah, the fidgeting. It's like my body just has to keep moving, even when I'm trying my hardest to just sit there and be still. My hands or my legs are always going.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Mind wandering? I mean, yeah, that's a big part of it, too. Like, when folks are talking to me, trying to tell me stuff I really need to know... like the ride times for the bus, or giving me a list for the food pantry, or even just plans for church on Sunday. I start out listening good, you know? I'm right there with 'em. But then... it's like my brain just slips off somewhere else. 

Like church, man. The stained glass windows in that place are amazing. All those colors when the sun hits 'em just right in the morning. Red, blue, that deep purple. Purple used to be for royalty, right? I read that somewhere. Only kings and queens could wear purple because the dye was so hard to get. They had to crush up these tiny little sea snails or something. Imagine crushing up thousands of snails just to get a purple shirt. Snails are weird. They leave that slimy trail everywhere they go. We had snails all over the sidewalk after it rained last week. You had to watch where you were walking or you'd hear that crunch. I hate that sound. Reminds me of eating potato chips, but not in a good way. I love potato chips, though. The barbecue kind, with the ridges. They hold the flavor better. 

Wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, my mind wandering. Yeah, so I'll be listening, and then I just drift off, and suddenly I realize I missed the most important pieces of what they were saying. Like the times or what I was supposed to bring. It's frustrating, man.

Completing tasks? I mean, yeah, it probably is. Like, I’ll start an errand for my aunt, or they'll give me a job to do down at the food pantry... usually sorting cans. Man, they get so many cans of green beans. Who even eats that many green beans? I always hope someone donates the canned peaches. The ones in the heavy syrup, you know? My grandma used to give me those. We had this big peach tree in her backyard. I used to climb up as high as I could go. Scraped my knees up so bad once, I had scabs for a month. Scabs are weird, right? How your body just builds a little crust to fix a hole. The human body is crazy. I watched this video on somebody's phone about how white blood cells attack germs, they just swallow 'em whole like Pac-Man. I used to play Pac-Man all the time at the arcade down the street before it closed down. Now it's just a mattress store. How many mattresses do people even need to buy? Seems like there's a mattress store on every single corner now. 

Wait, what was the question? Oh, completing tasks. Right. So yeah, I'll start the errand or the pantry job, and I'll do the first part just fine, but then... I don't know, I just completely forget the rest of it. I wander off or start doing something else. So then people find out later that I never actually finished it. My aunt gets pretty upset when she finds her groceries just sitting halfway unpacked on the kitchen counter hours later.

We used to go fishing out there all the time when we were kids. He had this little aluminum johnboat, and we'd take it out right around sunrise when the water was real still, like glass. Caught a bass out there once that must have been, I don't know, five pounds at least. Jimmy tried to tell everyone he caught it, but I know it was on my line. I feel like I just keep letting everybody down, even when I'm really trying to help.\n\nTrying to help... man, that reminds me of this time I tried to help my buddy fix his roof. Tar went everywhere, all down the side of the house, ruined his wife's petunias. She loves those petunias, plants 'em every spring. Spring is my favorite time of year, honestly. The weather finally warms up, and the baseball season starts. I used to play baseball when I was younger, played right field. I had this glove, this old leather Rawlings glove that my grandpa gave me. He bought it at a garage sale for like two dollars. Garage sales are great, you can find all sorts of stuff. Last week I found this old toaster oven, one of those retro ones from the 70s, bright orange. I brought it home to make toast, but the heating element was busted. So now it just sits on my counter looking pretty. \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, my mind wandering and the restlessness. Yeah, it's just... it's hard, you know? I just blurt it out, and then I realize I answered the wrong thing entirely or just made 'em mad. \n\nMade 'em mad... that makes me think of Mrs. Higgins at the pantry. She gets so mad if the soup cans ain't lined up right on the shelf. She wants every single label facing the exact same way. Reminds me of this guy I used to work for at a grocery store back when I was a teenager. He wore these thick glasses that made his eyes look huge, like a bug. We used to call him the Praying Mantis. Praying mantises are crazy bugs, though. I saw one on my porch last summer grab a moth right out of the air. Just snatched it. My porch light attracts all kinds of bugs in the summer. I tried putting up one of those blue bug zappers, but the noise it makes is just awful. Zzzap, zzzap. Sounds like a bad wire. I had a bad wire in my truck's radio once, meant I could only pick up AM stations. I ended up listening to a lot of talk radio back then... mostly guys calling in to complain about the weather. The weather's been so weird lately, ain't it? Supposed to rain all next week, I heard. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Something about talking? Oh, interrupting. Yeah, I just jump in before they're done. It just slips out before I can stop it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you have more trouble with paying attention or with sitting still and being quiet?\n\nPatient: Sitting still or paying attention? Man, I don't know, they're both pretty bad. Maybe the sitting still part? I just feel like I got this motor in me that won't shut off, you know? Like I always gotta be doing something or moving around. \n\nMoving around... that reminds me of this old rocking chair my grandma had. She'd sit on the porch and just rock back and forth all afternoon. It had this squeak to it, real high-pitched. Squeak, squeak. Drove my grandpa crazy. He tried oiling it, taking it apart, everything. Nothing worked. He was pretty handy, though. Built a whole shed in the backyard out of scrap wood he found at the dump. The dump is a wild place if you really look around. People throw away perfectly good stuff! I found a whole set of golf clubs there once. Never played golf in my life, but I figured I might try it. Took 'em to the park and ended up hitting a ball right through the window of this guy's minivan. He was not happy. Had to pay him like two hundred bucks to fix it. Two hundred bucks is a lot of money when you don't got a job, let me tell you. I try to save where I can, clip coupons and stuff. The Sunday paper always has the best coupons. I like reading the comics, too. Garfield is my favorite. He just hates Mondays and loves lasagna. \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, sitting still. Yeah, I don't know, I just can't seem to stay put for very long. It's like I'm always itching to get up and go somewhere else.\n\nDoctor: Do you often do things on impulse without considering the consequences?\n\nPatient: Impulse? Consequences? I ain't really sure what you mean by that, honestly. Like, I don't know if I do things without thinking. Maybe? I guess I just haven't really noticed if I do. Like buying stuff on impulse? Sometimes I grab a candy bar at the checkout line, if that counts. Usually a Snickers. I love the peanuts in 'em. Peanuts always make me think of going to baseball games with my uncle... well, minor league games, down at the old stadium before they tore it down. They had this mascot, a giant chicken or something. The guy inside that suit must've been sweating bullets, 'cause it gets so hot here in July. July is when my birthday is, actually. I didn't do much this year, just had a hot dog and watched TV. TV ain't what it used to be, though. It's all these reality shows now. I saw one about guys digging for gold up in Alaska. Alaska looks freezing, man. I don't think I could handle the cold up there, my knees start aching the second it drops below fifty degrees here. The free clinic doc told me it's just from getting older, but I ain't even forty yet. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Something about doing things on impulse? Yeah, I don't really know. I haven't really noticed doing anything like that, to be honest.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself doing things without fully thinking them through?\n\nPatient: Thinking them through? I don't know, maybe. I don't really think about how I think, if that makes sense. It's just... I do what I do. Like yesterday, I just started walking. Ended up down by the river. \n\nThe river's been real high lately, muddy too. Saw this guy fishing down there, had a whole setup with three poles and a little cooler. I asked him what he was catching, and he said catfish, mostly. Catfish are weird-looking, ain't they? With those whiskers. My buddy used to have a pet catfish in a big tank in his living room. It ate everything. He'd drop hot dogs in there, pieces of cheese, whatever. He had this other tank with a turtle, a red-eared slider. That turtle was mean, tried to bite my finger once. Fingers bleed a lot when you cut 'em, you ever notice that? I sliced mine open trying to open a can of baked beans last week. The pull-tab broke off, so I was using a screwdriver to pry it open. Stupid, I know, but I was hungry. Baked beans are best if you put a little mustard in 'em. My mom taught me that. She used to make this huge pot of beans for the church picnics every summer. Church picnics always had three-legged races. I was never any good at those, my legs are too long or something. I'm pretty tall, almost six-two. \n\nHold on, what were we talking about? Doing things without thinking? Yeah, I don't know. I guess I just do things when they come to me. I don't really sit around planning stuff out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you have more trouble with staying focused on tasks or with controlling your physical activity and restlessness?\n\nPatient: Staying focused or the restlessness? Man, that's tough. I mean, the moving around is bad, but the focus thing... yeah, that's a real problem too. Like, when folks are talking to me about stuff, I try to listen, I really do. Like if someone's running through the ride times for the bus, or reading off a pantry list, or even going over the plans for Sunday at church. I start out listening good, but then my mind just sort of... slips off. \n\nSlips off... like this time I was trying to fix the chain on my buddy's bike. It kept slipping off the gears. He had this old ten-speed, Schwinn I think it was. Had those curved handlebars that make you lean way over. I never liked those, hurts my back. My lower back has been killing me lately. I think it's from sleeping on this old mattress I got from my neighbor. He was moving to Florida, going to live with his daughter. Florida's got too many bugs for me. Palmetto bugs, they call 'em, but they're just giant cockroaches if you ask me. I saw one on TV once that was the size of a mouse. Mice are smart, though. You ever try to catch one? They figure out the traps real quick. You gotta use peanut butter, not cheese. Cheese dries out too fast. I like cheddar cheese on my eggs in the morning. Scrambled, with a little hot sauce. Hot sauce makes everything better. I got this bottle of habanero sauce that'll burn your tongue right off. \n\nAnyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, the focus thing. Yeah, my mind just wanders off, and then suddenly I realize I missed the pieces I really needed to hear. Like, I won't know what time the bus is coming or what I was supposed to bring to the church thing. It's frustrating, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your trouble with following instructions and paying attention is more of a problem for you than your restlessness and need to move?\n\nPatient: Following instructions? Man, I don't know if it's worse than the moving around, but it definitely gets me in trouble. Like, I’ll start an errand for my aunt or a job down at the food pantry, right? And I start out strong, but I end up doing just the first part and then... I just forget the rest. So people find out later that I never finished it. \n\nLike last week, my aunt asked me to go to the hardware store for some lightbulbs and a new air filter. Hardware stores are great, all those tools and stuff. They had this whole display of power washers right up front. I always wanted a power washer. You ever watch those videos of people power washing dirty driveways? It's real satisfying, just blasting all the grime away. Grime gets everywhere around here, especially with all the cars going by. The street sweepers come through on Tuesdays, but they don't do much. They just push the dirt around. I used to sweep the sidewalk in front of my old apartment building, just to keep it looking nice. The landlord there was a real piece of work, though. Never fixed anything. The radiator in my bedroom hissed all winter. Sounded like a snake. Snakes give me the creeps. Saw a garter snake in the park once, near the bushes. It was just a little guy, but I still jumped back. \n\nWait, what was the question? Instructions? Yeah, so I got the lightbulbs, but I completely forgot the air filter. Didn't even realize it until I got back and my aunt asked where it was. It just completely slipped my mind after I saw the power washers.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your trouble with paying attention is worse than your trouble with sitting still and being quiet?\n\nPatient: Worse than sitting still? I don't know if it's worse, man. The sitting still part is... it's like a physical thing, you know? Like, I just can't do it. If I'm in a meeting down at the community center, or eating dinner with my aunt, or even just sitting in a waiting room like out there... I just keep standing up. I gotta go get water, or I go look out the window to see what's happening outside, or I just pace around a little. Even when everyone else is just sitting there, quiet, staring at me like I'm crazy. \n\nStaring at me... people stare a lot. Like this guy at the bus stop the other day, just staring at my shoes. I got these new sneakers, bright orange. Orange is a good color, easy to see in the dark. You gotta be careful walking around at night, people drive like maniacs. My cousin got hit by a car once, broke his leg in three places. He had this massive cast on for months. Everyone signed it. I drew a little picture of a dog on it. I always wanted a dog, like a big one, a German Shepherd maybe. They use those for police dogs, right? Smart dogs. I saw a police dog sniff out a whole bag of stuff hidden in a car bumper on one of those cop shows. Those shows are intense. All the sirens and running. \n\nBut yeah, what were you asking? The sitting still thing? Yeah, it's bad. I just can't stay in my chair. I'm always up and down, up and down, getting water or just moving. It's like my legs have a mind of their own.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you have more trouble with staying focused on what people are saying or with staying still and not fidgeting?\n\nPatient: Staying focused or fidgeting? Man, they're both just... they're always there. But the fidgeting, it's like I can't even stop it if I try. Even when I'm really trying to be respectful, like if I'm talking to my aunt's pastor or something, my leg is just bouncing up and down, up and down. Or my fingers are tapping on the chair, or I'm twisting whatever I can get my hands on, like a pen or a piece of paper. \n\nPaper... my aunt has all these old newspapers stacked up in the corner of the living room. She says she's gonna read 'em, but she never does. They just sit there collecting dust. Dust is bad for your lungs, you know? I saw this documentary about coal miners and how they get that black lung disease. Sounds awful. Being down in a mine all day, in the dark, with all that dust. I don't like tight spaces anyway. I got stuck in an elevator once for like, two hours. Just me and this lady who kept talking about her cats. She had six cats. Six! That's too many cats. The smell alone... I can't stand the smell of litter boxes. \n\nBut yeah, the fidgeting. It's like my body just has to keep moving, even when I'm trying my hardest to just sit there and be still. My hands or my legs are always going.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind wandering and trouble following instructions is the main issue you're dealing with?\n\nPatient: Mind wandering? I mean, yeah, that's a big part of it, too. Like, when folks are talking to me, trying to tell me stuff I really need to know... like the ride times for the bus, or giving me a list for the food pantry, or even just plans for church on Sunday. I start out listening good, you know? I'm right there with 'em. But then... it's like my brain just slips off somewhere else. \n\nLike church, man. The stained glass windows in that place are amazing. All those colors when the sun hits 'em just right in the morning. Red, blue, that deep purple. Purple used to be for royalty, right? I read that somewhere. Only kings and queens could wear purple because the dye was so hard to get. They had to crush up these tiny little sea snails or something. Imagine crushing up thousands of snails just to get a purple shirt. Snails are weird. They leave that slimy trail everywhere they go. We had snails all over the sidewalk after it rained last week. You had to watch where you were walking or you'd hear that crunch. I hate that sound. Reminds me of eating potato chips, but not in a good way. I love potato chips, though. The barbecue kind, with the ridges. They hold the flavor better. \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, my mind wandering. Yeah, so I'll be listening, and then I just drift off, and suddenly I realize I missed the most important pieces of what they were saying. Like the times or what I was supposed to bring. It's frustrating, man.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your trouble with paying attention is the main reason you have difficulty completing tasks?\n\nPatient: Completing tasks? I mean, yeah, it probably is. Like, I’ll start an errand for my aunt, or they'll give me a job to do down at the food pantry... usually sorting cans. Man, they get so many cans of green beans. Who even eats that many green beans? I always hope someone donates the canned peaches. The ones in the heavy syrup, you know? My grandma used to give me those. We had this big peach tree in her backyard. I used to climb up as high as I could go. Scraped my knees up so bad once, I had scabs for a month. Scabs are weird, right? How your body just builds a little crust to fix a hole. The human body is crazy. I watched this video on somebody's phone about how white blood cells attack germs, they just swallow 'em whole like Pac-Man. I used to play Pac-Man all the time at the arcade down the street before it closed down. Now it's just a mattress store. How many mattresses do people even need to buy? Seems like there's a mattress store on every single corner now. \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, completing tasks. Right. So yeah, I'll start the errand or the pantry job, and I'll do the first part just fine, but then... I don't know, I just completely forget the rest of it. I wander off or start doing something else. So then people find out later that I never actually finished it. My aunt gets pretty upset when she finds her groceries just sitting halfway unpacked on the kitchen counter hours later.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
